E.l.f. Beauty posts 51% sales increase, raises full-year guidance

📅 2026-11-04  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

E.l.f. Beauty on Wednesday blew past Wall Street expectations as it posted a 51% increase in net sales in its second fiscal quarter.

The beauty company said it saw its 31st consecutive quarter of net sales growth, attributing the streak to strong performance across its portfolio. The revenue spike was driven by both retail and e-commerce channels across the U.S. and internationally, the company added.

"The thing I'm most pleased about is we saw strength across our entire portfolio," CEO Tarang Amin told CNBC in an interview. "Every one of our brands contributed strong growth this quarter."

Amin added that E.l.f. is the most-searched brand on TikTok Shop and the No. 2 brand in cosmetics on the platform. The company also said it saw strong results for Rhode, the skincare brand it acquired last year.

The company raised its full fiscal-year guidance following the better-than-anticipated quarter. It now expects net sales in the range of $2 billion to $2.03 billion, compared with a previous range of between $1.94 billion and $1.97 billion. It also upped its adjusted EBITDA projection to a range of $421 million to $427 million from between $401 million and $407 million.

E.l.f. said it now expects adjusted earnings per share for the year to come in between $3.82 and $3.86, exceeding prior guidance of between $3.50 and $3.55 per share. It's also expecting a net income range of $231 million to $233 million, up from a previous range of $212 million to $215 million.

It had already raised its guidance last quarter after outperforming its expectations.

Amin said the company is "very bullish" on the holiday season, expecting high sales through the end of the year.

Here's how the company performed in its second fiscal quarter compared with what Wall Street was expecting, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

For the period ended Sept. 30, E.l.f. reported net income of $30.03 million, or 50 cents per share, up significantly from roughly $3 million, or 5 cents per share, the year prior.

E.l.f. also saw significant growth in its international channels, with business up 165%. Amin told CNBC that only 25% of the company's business is outside of the U.S. and that it has a "tremendous opportunity" to build on that momentum.

The company also said its gross margin increased 1.6% during the quarter, including a 0.3% benefit from tariff refunds. Amin previously said the retailer planned to fully reinvest that money into pricing and marketing to continue driving growth.

Though E.l.f. received most of its refunds in its first fiscal quarter, some of those funds flowed through the second quarter, and the company expects to receive a yearly total of around $55 million. Amin told CNBC on Wednesday that the company lowered the price of about 10% of its units by $1 because of the tariff benefits.

"We're seeing great unit momentum based on that," he said.

Palmer Harding Spring 2027 Ready-to-Wear

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

As much as anniversaries are cause for celebration, they naturally also prompt introspection. For Palmer Harding, this season marks 15 years in operation—and their 60th full collection. These are milestones well worth raising a glass to for any independent brand, not least one that has weathered the market instabilities and headwinds that we’ll take your understanding of as a given.

The past year has prompted particularly significant operational developments for the brand – a noteworthy one being the opening of a retail space within its East London studio. In part prompted by the widely attested decline of global wholesale retail, this direct-to-consumer pivot has allowed for an intimate atmosphere that, Matthew Harding says, “feels almost like when we first started; there’s more interaction between us, the clothes and the customer.”

This, in itself, became something of a prompt for their latest collection; less a nostalgic ‘greatest hits’ offering, more a zeroing in on the pieces they’ve seen resonate. “It’s very much a collection about refining and celebrating our key pieces, translating them into new silhouettes, new categories,” Harding adds.

Contemplating what exactly those pieces were didn’t take them too long. “When we actually hunkered down and said, ‘Okay, what are we about?’ The answer was cotton shirts and shirt dresses. When we find a shape, let's just really hammer it home," Palmer says, holding up a hefty poplin button-down with articulated pleating along its oversized sleeve, creating an easy, boomerang bend. First introduced two seasons ago, here, the aptly-named “Intention” shirt returns across a gamut of plain and striped cottons, and as a full-length dress imbued with a satisfying, fanning swish.

Elsewhere, the asymmetrically-hemmed “Super” shirt—“one of our hero pieces that really launched us way back when,” Palmer recalls, “but that we haven't had in the collection for a long time”—showcases the duo’s knack for sculptural cut and drape with its tie-front and cape-like back. Though the piece that showcases that most intriguingly is what Harding dubs the “Precision” shirt, which features origami-like criss-cross pleating at the waist to create a line that skims the form.

There are, granted, more than crisp cotton shirts and shirt dresses on offer here. A denim riff on the “Intention” silhouette stands out, as does a ruby satin dress with swooping poet sleeves. Their insistent focus on their chosen fabric and garment, however, is in itself somewhat allegorical of the alchemical formula that’s spurred their trajectory over the past 15 years. “There's something really wonderful about taking what’s seen as a boring, everyday fabric and elevating it in a really sculptural way,” Palmer says. “The result is something this blend of something that's really understandable, but also very special.” A decade and a half on from setting out with that mission, the eclectic array of women willing to journey far beyond London’s well-trodden luxury shopping lanes to try and buy their wares in person proves that still holds true.

Raia Drogasil S.A. (RADLY) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Judge Lets Stock Perpetual Futures Keep Trading but Says CFTC Likely Overstepped Its Authority

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

A federal judge refused to halt stock perpetual futures on Kalshi, Coinbase Derivatives and Bitnomial but found a Cboe- and Citadel Securities-backed group likely to prove the CFTC exceeded its authority.

Stock perpetual futures can keep trading on Kalshi, Coinbase Derivatives and Bitnomial for now after a federal judge denied an emergency bid to halt them Tuesday, while finding that the Wall Street-backed challengers are likely to prove the CFTC overstepped its authority.

U.S. District Judge Valerie Caproni denied the temporary restraining order on Nov. 3 after a teleconference with the parties, hours after the CFTC said the products could begin trading on U.S.-regulated exchanges.

The plaintiff is the Capital Markets Alliance Association, a New York nonprofit whose members include Cboe Global Markets, Citadel Securities and the Healthy Markets Association, according to a declaration from its secretary, Adam Ford.

The group sued the CFTC and Chairman Michael S. Selig in the Southern District of New York, case No. 1:26-cv-09608, seeking to undo the approvals of filings by all three exchanges.

Caproni wrote that the group “is likely to succeed on the merits” of its claim that the agency lacks statutory authority to allow perpetual derivatives on individual stocks to be offered to retail investors on CFTC-regulated platforms.

“Because the perpetual contracts, by definition, do not provide for a ‘sale for future delivery of a single security,’ they cannot be a security future over which the CFTC has statutory and regulatory authority,” she wrote.

Instead, she said, the contracts “appear to satisfy the definition of a swap,” which falls to the Securities and Exchange Commission.

She also found that the balance of equities and the public interest favor the alliance, writing that the agency’s actions “seem to represent a major disruption to the equities market.”

The request failed on one prong. Caproni said the declarations from Cboe, Citadel Securities and Healthy Markets were “conjectural” about the harm members would suffer and did not show irreparable harm that was “actual and imminent.”

The order leaves the approvals in place. It does not decide the case or the alliance’s separate motion for a preliminary injunction.

The CFTC’s opposition is due Friday, Nov. 6, and the alliance’s reply is due Tuesday, Nov. 10. The order says that schedule is subject to change by the assigned judge. The docket lists Judge Lorna G. Schofield as assigned to the case.

Kalshi was already listing four single-stock perps on Qualcomm, McDonald’s, Amgen and Costco by Wednesday morning, with maximum leverage of 4.9 times.

Bloomberg Law reported that the judge signaled she might ultimately side with the Wall Street firms opposed to the products.

The fight is over a label. Selig’s Nov. 3 statement said the contracts were deemed approved under Commission Regulation 40.3 as security futures products, a category the CFTC and SEC oversee jointly. The exchanges filed them Sept. 18, and the agency let its 45-day review period lapse without a written order.

The alliance argues that a contract with no expiration date and no delivery obligation is a security-based swap. Congress limited retail trading in those to national securities exchanges under full SEC oversight.

Its complaint says the CFTC “invited a parallel, synthetic market in U.S. stocks to grow outside the exclusive jurisdiction” of the SEC.

A CFTC spokesperson called the lawsuit “troubling” and told Reuters: “Our markets should be governed by law, not litigation campaigns intended to block innovation.”

For crypto-native venues, the stakes reach past stocks. Perpetuals are the core product of crypto derivatives trading, and Kalshi and Coinbase have used the CFTC’s framework to bring them onshore.

The agency approved the first U.S.-listed bitcoin perpetual on May 29, and Kalshi went live with the product soon after.

That approval already faces a challenge. CME Group sued the CFTC in June, arguing that a contract built on exchanged funding payments is a swap, not a future. That case is pending in Washington.

The New York order covers security futures, not commodities like bitcoin. But a federal judge has now said, at least preliminarily, that a contract with no expiration does not look like a future.

GomSpace Strengthens Global Commercial Organization with Appointment of New Vice Presidents of Sales for APAC and EMEA

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Appointments reinforce GomSpace's global growth strategy and strengthen regional customer engagement across Europe, the Middle East, Africa, and Asia-Pacific.

STOCKHOLM, Nov. 4, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- GomSpace, announces the appointment of Anand Iqbal Gulati as Vice President Sales, APAC and Benjamin Hamel as Vice President Sales, EMEA. The appointments mark a significant step in GomSpace's continued commitment to strengthening its global commercial presence and supporting customers across key international markets.

As part of its evolving commercial strategy, GomSpace has established dedicated sales leadership for three regions: North America, EMEA (Europe, Middle East and Africa), and APAC (Asia-Pacific). The new regional structure is designed to bring the company closer to customers, enhance market responsiveness, and support the growing demand for advanced space solutions worldwide.

Anand Iqbal Gulati joins GomSpace as Vice President Sales, APAC, bringing extensive experience in sales, business development, and strategic market expansion across Asia. Based in Singapore, Anand has built a strong track record working with government, defense, national security, and commercial organizations throughout the region. Prior to joining GomSpace, he served as Head of Sales, Cyber Solutions APAC at Rheinmetall, where he consistently exceeded sales targets and developed a robust regional business pipeline. He has also held senior sales leadership roles with Xolaris and SES Satellites, giving him deep insight into the space and satellite sectors.

"Asia-Pacific represents one of the most dynamic and rapidly growing space markets in the world," says Carsten Drachmann, CEO of GomSpace. "Anand's extensive regional experience, strong customer relationships, and proven ability to grow businesses make him exceptionally well positioned to lead our expansion across APAC. We are delighted to welcome him to the GomSpace team."

Benjamin Hamel has been appointed Vice President Sales, EMEA. With more than 20 years of experience spanning aerospace, space technology, aviation, and international business development, Benjamin brings extensive commercial leadership expertise to GomSpace. Throughout his career, he has successfully led growth initiatives, strategic partnerships, and customer engagement programs across global markets. His background includes leadership roles within the aerospace and technology sectors, where he developed a reputation for building high-performing commercial organizations and driving sustainable business growth.

"Benjamin's impressive international experience and customer-centric approach will be invaluable as we continue to strengthen our position across EMEA," says Carsten Drachmann. "His understanding of complex technology markets and ability to develop long-term strategic relationships align perfectly with GomSpace's ambitions for future growth."

The appointments underscore GomSpace's commitment to investing in commercial excellence as the company continues to support customers in the defense, security, scientific, and commercial space sectors. With dedicated regional leadership teams now in place, GomSpace is well positioned to accelerate growth, deepen customer engagement, and expand access to its flight-proven space technologies across global markets.

The company's business operations are mainly conducted through the wholly owned Danish subsidiary, GomSpace A/S, with headquarters and operational facilities in Aalborg, Denmark. GomSpace also has key operations in Luxembourg, France, and the United States. GomSpace develops and delivers advanced systems and services that enable governments, commercial enterprises, and research institutions to achieve their objectives in space. The company's expertise covers satellite subsystems, complete small satellite missions, and satellite operations, providing solutions that support smarter, faster, and more affordable access to space. Reaching Full Retirement Age for Social Security in 2027? Here's Why You May Not Want to File Just Yet

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Taking benefits right away isn't your only option.

If you'll be turning 67 in 2027, you should know that you'll be hitting a big milestone in the context of Social Security. Age 67 is Social Security's full retirement age. That's when you can collect your monthly benefits based on your earnings history without a reduction.

The earliest age to claim Social Security is actually 62. But for each month you take benefits before reaching full retirement age, those monthly checks are reduced. So if you were born in 1960 or later, waiting until 67 to claim Social Security can be a smart move.

But that doesn't mean you should rush to claim Social Security next year if you'll be turning 67. You may find that waiting makes even more sense.

While full retirement age is when you get your Social Security checks without a reduction, delaying your claim past that point boosts your monthly benefits by 8% a year for life. And while this incentive does run out at age 70, if you hold off on filing for three years, you can boost your Social Security benefits by 24%.

Imagine your full retirement age benefit is $2,000 a month. Waiting until age 70 to file gives you $2,480 per month instead. And that's not the only perk.

Each year, Social Security benefits are eligible for a cost-of-living adjustment, or COLA. If you boost your benefits by waiting past full retirement age to file, each COLA that comes through will be worth more money to you by virtue of having a larger monthly baseline check.

Also, if you're the higher earner in your household and your spouse outlives you, they'll typically be entitled to survivor benefits from Social Security after you pass away. The larger your benefits are, the more money your spouse stands to collect.

If 2027 is the year you'll reach full retirement age for Social Security, you may be inclined to take benefits at that time. But there's a lot to be gained by waiting longer to file.

Even if delaying your claim until age 70 isn't feasible, holding off for a single year results in an 8% boost. And a smaller increase could help your retirement finances, too.

Remember, the more Social Security pays you each month, the less pressure there is on your savings. Keep that and the points above in mind when your 67th birthday arrives so you don't make a filing decision you regret.

Wolves can laugh to the bank after Leeds United transfer decision

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Wolves had a hectic summer as Cesar Peixoto surprisingly replaced Rob Edwards as head coach, although the major squad overhaul that many expected didn’t exactly happen.

Edwards had been keen to make major changes to the group following Wanderers’ embarrassing relegation, but those plans were ripped up after the ex-Gil Vicente chief arrived at Molineux.

Peixoto was willing to work with many of the current group, so Wolves opted to keep a large chunk of the players that had struggled in the top flight.

However, it’s worth noting that in some cases, the decision would’ve been made for the hierarchy, as offers just didn’t arrive. But, one player that did attract attention was Ladislav Krejčí, yet Wolves managed to retain the Czech captain.

The 27-year-old joined Wolves from Girona last summer on an initial loan that included an obligation to make the move permanent for around £26m.

So, it was a significant outlay, and whilst Krejci wasn’t exactly brilliant in the Premier League, he was one of few members of the Wolves squad that emerged with any credit from that forgettable campaign.

Football League World takes a look at the current betting odds at the top end of the Championship.

Therefore, there was an expectation that Krejci would leave this summer, particularly as he played a key role in helping his country to the World Cup as well.

Leeds were credited with an interest, but they ultimately signed Tarik Muharemovic as their left-footed centre-back, whilst PSV’s late offer worth up to £18m was understandably turned down.

The lack of serious bids for Krejci may have shocked figures at Wolves, because the former Sparta Prague man does have good pedigree, having done well in La Liga prior to his decent season in the Premier League.

Once Leeds decided to look elsewhere, it was always going to be difficult for clubs overseas to stump up the £26m that would have given Wolves their money back for the defender.

You can understand why PSV tried to test Wolves’ resolve with a late £18m offer, and there may have been a temptation to cash in considering those funds could have been used elsewhere.

But, it wouldn’t have been a good deal for Wolves, and even though Fosun have rightly come in for a lot of criticism, they deserve credit for sticking to their guns on Krejci’s valuation, especially as the player would have been keen to play at a higher level.

Krejci didn’t feature for Wolves for their first four league games as speculation continued over his future, but, once the window was shut, he was straight back into the XI at Birmingham - where he scored in a 2-2 draw.

Peixoto’s eagerness to rotate meant the defender, like everyone else, was in and out of the team over the next month or so, but it now appears he has earned his place in the best XI, having started three games on the bounce.

Unsurprisingly, that continuity has helped Krejci and Wolves, with the centre-back having put in back-to-back commanding displays against Watford and Cardiff, with the side recording consecutive clean sheets.

Krejci was particularly good against the Bluebirds, where he capped off his fine individual display with another goal, as he nodded in from a Kieran Trippier corner.

As well as his defensive qualities, Krejci’s ability on the ball is crucial to the way Peixoto wants to play, as he is capable of firing passes between the lines, as well as switching play.

With Krejci captain of his country, he also brings leadership qualities that Peixoto has highlighted, which is further proof that the boss is pleased with what he is bringing to the team right now.

Five players, five careers. Name them from the clubs they played for.

Each day there are five current or former EFL players to identify. For each one you’ll see their senior clubs in the order they played for them. Loan spells are left out.

You get four guesses per player. Every wrong guess unlocks a clue: first their position, then their nationality, then their initials.

Get it first time for 4 points, then 3, 2 and 1. The best possible score is 20. A new set of players arrives at midnight.

Obviously, the major concern for Wolves is that January just brings about more uncertainty surrounding Krejci’s future, as his form won’t have gone unnoticed.

But, providing Wolves are in the hunt for promotion, which looks likely, then Fosun must once again insist that Krejci isn’t for sale unless stupid money is put on the table.

If he was to leave, he would need to be replaced, and the reality is that Wolves won’t be able to buy an upgrade on Krejci at this level, and the money he would bring in is nothing compared to the worth of promotion.

What price should Wolves put on Ladislav Krejčí in January? Let us know in the comments

Plus, you would hope that Krejci acknowledges Wolves’ position, and the fact he has got his head down and concentrated on his football shows he isn’t desperate to leave.

Of course, if Wolves fail to win promotion, then the player would be sold, but there should be an understanding between Krejci and the club that they will revisit the situation in the summer.

Wolves will feel a return to the Premier League is a real possibility, and, if they do bounce back at the first attempt, Krejci is likely to play a big part, so there will be huge relief that the likes of Leeds and PSV didn’t make a stronger push for the defender in the last window.

What Trump losing power over Congress means for Iran, Ukraine and foreign policy

📅 2026-11-04  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

With Democrats taking control of Congress, U.S. President Donald Trump could intensify his focus on foreign policy in his final two years in office, while facing greater scrutiny over the wars in Iran and Ukraine, experts told CNBC.

The Associated Press is projecting Democrats will have a Senate majority, flipping seats from Republicans in Iowa, Kansas, North Carolina, Ohio, South Carolina, and Texas.

Democrats also won control of the House, giving the party greater scope to scrutinize the administration, shape spending and challenge Trump's foreign-policy agenda.

"We are going to see a president that is somewhat constrained on matters of oversight and investigation, but on foreign policy, he does still retain a lot of latitude," Laurel Rapp, director of Chatham House's U.S. and North America Programme, told CNBC's "Squawk Box Europe."

Rapp said the Iran war was a "major issue" in this election cycle amid soaring fuel prices, food costs, and inflation. The war began when Trump launched joint strikes with Israel on Feb. 28.

"Republicans don't really have an off-ramp here at this stage. We know the Democrats will seek to take votes in the House and the Senate to constrain the Republicans, to restrain the administration on Iran war powers. But whether that will bind the administration is another matter," Rapp added.

She said that Democrats could exercise the "power of the purse" to limit spending on U.S. military and other Iran-related costs.

With control of both chambers, Democrats could pass legislation seeking to direct Trump to end U.S. involvement in the conflict. However, making such a measure binding over a presidential veto would require enough votes in Congress to override it.

J.C. Lintzenich, visiting senior fellow at the German Marshall Fund, said that the president is not a "lame duck."

"Remember that this election changes the Congress; it does not change the president," Lintzenich told CNBC's "Squawk Box Europe" on Wednesday.

"The President of the United States still has lots of power, lots of authority. We still have pending action in Iran. We still have an unresolved situation with Russia and Ukraine."

He added that while Congress has changed, foreign policy is still executed by the president and his appointed officials.

"What we'll now see is a little bit more congressional oversight, the application of funding, for example, Ukraine, that's required by statute and law," Lintzenich said.

Lintzenich said the outgoing Congress was "more willing to let the White House slide," but Democratic control of both chambers would mean a more assertive Congress, using its Article I powers over legislation, spending, and oversight to hold the White House more closely to existing law and congressional requirements.

West Ham took too long to sack Nuno and now must trust Parker to do what he does best | Jacob Steinberg

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Forget speculation over how a Parker-led side would fare in the Premier League and let the man who has taken three clubs up do his work again

It has taken West Ham six months to come to their senses and sack Nuno Espírito Santo, but at least the decision has arrived early enough to revive their hopes of automatic promotion.

Dithering was no longer an option after the dismal performance against West Brom last Saturday.

West Ham, eighth in the Championship and four points off the top two, had to act after a second consecutive home defeat. They are in danger of slipping into a battle for a playoff spot and needed to move on from Nuno after just over a year of his confused, passive football.

Nuno did a terrible job after replacing Graham Potter as manager in September 2025. The 52-year-old had 33 games to save West Ham from relegation and failed. He has the most expensive squad in the Championship but his side have not lived up to their billing as title favourites and their stuttering form has exposed the folly of not sacking Nuno at the end of last season.

David Sullivan, then the club’s largest shareholder, wanted a change. But Daniel Kretinsky favoured continuity and looked to capitalise on the shifting power dynamics in the boardroom. Karren Brady had stepped down as vice-chair in April – Karim Virani replaced her in an interim role – and there was further upheaval when Sullivan resigned as joint-chair and a director of the club to fight what he described as “false allegations” regarding his personal conduct.

Sullivan bears most culpability for West Ham’s relegation. He still owns a 40% stake but is no longer on the board and West Ham are better off without him dictating key decisions. Equally, though, where Sullivan was not mistaken was in wanting Nuno gone. Kretinsky got his first big call wrong. At least the Czech billionaire has given Nuno’s replacement time to get the team back on track. The task falls to Scott Parker, who has been on the market since leaving Burnley last April. Parker is regarded as a “safe” appointment and has been on West Ham’s radar for a while.

Some wanted the former England midfielder in the summer and his name was mentioned again when West Ham failed to win their first three league games. Patience was wearing thin. A subsequent run of four wins in a row swiftly proved a false dawn.

West Ham have not had the right mindset. They have two wins from their past six games and have often started playing only after going a goal down. A 6-0 win against Wrexham was a highlight but victory at Lincoln by the same scoreline came with the caveat that the Imps received two red cards before half-time.

There was no identity under Nuno. West Ham are unbeaten on the road and won 1-0 at the then table-topping Swansea last month but it has been hard to tell Nuno broke the Football League transfer record with the £22m signing of the Belgium midfielder Arne Engels from Celtic. Even Jarrod Bowen, who was convinced to sign a new deal in the summer, has been off colour. Nuno’s tactics mean the winger, West Ham’s top scorer, is often crowded out.

West Ham were lopsided against West Brom. Enough was enough. Kretinsky, who is not at every game, was unimpressed when he attended West Ham’s 3-2 defeat by Southampton a week earlier. He pays attention to the views of Bowen, Tomas Soucek and Mark Noble, the technical director and former club captain.

Nuno, whose substitutions were almost as weird as his starting lineups, had lost the players. The mood darkened after the West Brom game. West Ham were easy to play through because of Nuno’s reluctance to use a holding midfielder, an issue exacerbated by his refusal to use Edson Álvarez or Soungoutou Magassa after both sought moves during the summer.

Magassa’s exile has caused disquiet. Not only is a young asset being devalued, a fragile defence is going without enough protection. West Ham have a lot of No 8s but they need a No 6. It is an area for Parker to address. He will also need to work out whether to stick with Mads Hermansen in goal, and the jury is out on summer signings.

One source questions whether West Ham targeted players equipped to handle the intensity of the Championship. Divine Mukasa, the 19-year-old midfielder, is talented but inconsistent. Manor Solomon and Edvin Austbø have flattered to deceive on the left wing. Joël Veltman, the 34-year-old Dutch right-back, has had fitness issues. Adama Boiro, a 24-year-old left‑back on loan from Athletic Bilbao, has not been seen since a cameo against Derby in early September. The three strikers signed by Nuno across two windows have toiled; Pablo Felipe, Taty Castellanos and Joël Piroe have five league goals between them this season.

Nils Koppen, the head of recruitment appointed in the summer, will have to be busy in January, but Parker will hope to have made his impact before the window opens. He is not known for expansive football but he is proven at this level. Parker won promotion with Bournemouth, Burnley and Fulham. The 46-year-old’s track record is clear and it helps that he has an association with West Ham.

Parker, whose all-action playing style made him a fan favourite, knows how difficult it can be in east London. He played in some pretty bad West Ham sides, experienced relegation in 2011 and arrives with the club in a tricky spot again.

It is not the moment to start worrying about Parker’s questionable record as a Premier League manager. What do West Ham do if they go up? Do they stick with Parker? Do they make an instant change? Well, how about they get promoted first? The rest can wait.

Gold, silver and precious metals prices jump, dollar dips as Democrats take control of Congress

📅 2026-11-04  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Precious metals saw strong gains on Tuesday morning, while the value of the dollar fell, following the U.S. midterm elections.

Spot gold was 2% higher at $4,005.40 an ounce by 1:30 a.m. ET, while spot silver jumped 4% to trade at $51.68 per ounce.

Front-month gold futures added 2.2% to trade at $4,020.60, while silver futures were up 3.8% at $51.53.

Other metals seeing notable gains on Wednesday morning included platinum, which jumped 3.3% to trade at $1,462.20, and palladium, which was 3.8% higher at $969.33.

The moves came as vote counting continued in the midterms, with the Democrats projected to have won control of the House and multiple governors' offices also turning blue.

U.S. stock futures broadly fell as the results came in, while Treasury yields also moved lower. Oil prices were marginally higher, while the U.S. dollar index shed around 0.3%.

Rhona O'Connell, head of market analysis for EMEA and Asia at StoneX, said in a note on Tuesday that if the Democrats won dual Congressional control, gold should find support as rates and the dollar would likely come under pressure.

She said it would result in "fiscal brinkmanship, institutional friction, both leading to uncertainty," adding that "the Iran war and a weaker dollar would compete with [the] impact of [a] hawkish Fed."

"Less fiscal stimulus argues for lower long yields and a softer dollar, offset by debt-limit premia in bills and a Fed leaning towards another hike," she said.

Both gold and silver enjoyed record-breaking rallies in 2025, surging 66% and 135% respectively over the course of the year as investors sought safe haven assets amid geopolitical uncertainty and broader market volatility.

But the momentum has come undone this year, with rising inflation, surging bond yields and hawkish central banks removing support from the metals. Gold is down more than 7% year-to-date, while silver has tumbled almost 28%.

Spot gold rose on Monday as oil prices retreated, and is now on course for a weekly rise of around 3.7%.

Daniela Hathorn, senior market analyst at Capital.com, said in a Tuesday note that the recovery remains fragile.

"The challenge for precious metals is that geopolitical uncertainty is not automatically translating into safe-haven demand when investors are also concerned about restrictive monetary policy," she said. "They also face the risk of liquidation during sharp equity selloffs, as investors sell profitable or liquid positions to raise cash."

PSG: Mika Godts’ time has finally arrived!

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

With the injuries to Ousmane Dembélé and Khvicha Kvaratskhelia, all eyes are naturally turning to Désiré Doué, expected to take more responsibility in the Parisian attack. But another player also had a good card to play: Mika Godts. Recruited this summer to replace, on paper, Bradley Barcola, the Belgian winger has so far struggled to convince. His entries into the game had often left a taste of unfinished business, to the point that the possibility of an offensive reinforcement was already mentioned to compensate for the absence of Kvaratskhelia. As if we still doubted the ability of his recruit to meet expectations. Facing Olympique Lyonnais this Monday evening, Godts therefore had an ideal opportunity to silence the first criticisms.

Still replacing at kick-off, the former Ajax player was launched while PSG came up against a particularly compact Lyon block. His first minutes, however, long resembled his previous appearances. Available, multiplying calls in depth, the Belgian was rarely served by his partners. But he never gave up, displaying constant involvement, including in defensive tasks. He notably stood out with a magnificent save on his line to prevent OL from scoring. The fact remains that Godts was expected offensively. And this time, he responded. First a decisive passer for Senny Mayulu on the opening score in Paris, he then scored his first goal in the colors of PSG with a clear right-footed strike which left Dominik Greif no chance.

A liberating performance for the Belgian winger, who did not hide his satisfaction in the mixed zone after the match. “It’s good to have scored and helped the team. I just wanted to help the team. What if I was waiting for this goal? Even before that, I am calm. I try to work every day. And when the coach needs me, I’m there to help the team. I’m happy, when you’re a striker you want to score and help with goals.”he explained. Happy to have finally unlocked his score in his new club, Godts also wanted to emphasize the importance of those around him in his adaptation. “It’s good to score here, I’m happy. I feel good in the team, they help me on and off the pitch. When I scored, I thought of my family, my wife, they are important to me and it is thanks to them that I can work hard. »

For his part, Senny Mayulu did not fail to praise the performance of his teammate, with whom he combined perfectly to open the score. “I’m happy for him, we have a good connection. But it’s with everyone, we’re a family. So I’m happy that Godts passed me and scored, it’ll do him good.”confided the young Parisian midfielder in the mixed zone. An evening which could mark a turning point in Mika Godts’ Parisian adventure. Until now struggling to find his place in the offensive rotation, the Belgian finally showed what he could bring to Luis Enrique’s collective. While PSG must deal with several major absences in attack, this performance comes at the best time. It is now up to him to confirm to prove that he can become a real offensive solution for the coming weeks.

INTERVIEW: Carsten Cramer relaxed about Dortmund's transfer practices despite failing to sign Köln's Said El Mala

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

MÜNCHEN (Bulinews): In Munich last weekend, Borussia Dortmund CEO Carsten Cramer stated the Westphalian club have not blown their cover after last summer’s prolonged transfer saga for 1.FC Köln star Said El Mala.

“From our perspective, we made a huge offer to them [Köln for El Mala] which maybe five years ago we wouldn’t have paid that much for a player,” Cramer explained at a round table event for which Bulinews.com was present.

“Numbers [in transfer fees] are changing at the moment, but we won’t pay ridiculous or stupid [prices] because we want to be a solid and serious club. No financial risks will be taken for successes.

The 20-year-old was heavily linked with BVB who, according to Sky Germany and BILD, offered fees of up to €50million but were rejected. In response to this, Cramer has no fears that the club’s historically cost-effective transfer methods have been exposed.

“We never mentioned the fee [for Said El Mala] publicly, so that [€50 million offer] is still a rumour. We just said we were interested to spend a huge amount of money,” he pointed out, casting doubt over their actual offer to the Cathedral City outfit.

“It can happen that [clubs will know Dortmund can now pay more for players] but that will not influence our way of negotiating. Everyone knows we are a powerful club, and we are a financially very healthy football club.

“We don’t need to hide our strengths. If we have an opportunity to spend money on a talented player which might sound a bit too expensive, if we are convinced, we might do it. I’m not concerned about [clubs knowing we can pay more],” he added.

Late last August, Köln would agree a contract extension with their talisman El Mala until June 2031. Could Dortmund plan another approach in the future? Cramer was quick to suppress the idea and avoid further rumours developing.

“We are happy with our squad; everyone is fully convinced with it. We decided to stop our negotiations with Köln because we had good ideas in mind and not only going down the El Mala route.

“It showed everyone that Dortmund is interested to make some special signings. What we have done with [Giannis] Konstantelias, [Kostas] Karetsas and [Joey] Veerman was really progressive from our perspective.

“We spent a huge amount of money for them, and don’t forget we have [Joane] Gadou who we hired very early from Salzburg. He may be cheap given his potential, but he cost money as well.

“No decision is done regarding how we proceed in the next transfer windows. We watch the market and there is no further comment regarding any player including El Mala, however his quality still exists,” Dortmund’s CEO concluded.

Personal Investments • Re: Portfolio review and using bonds in taxable when 401k space is full

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →


Questions:
=========

1. Taxable bonds:
Bonds are divided as 20% in 401k + 6% in taxable bonds = 26% bonds in portfolio. All new 401k contributions are added as bonds but using taxable bonds too as 401k space is full.
Is my taxable bond choices okay, or should I do something different? Currently using Muni bonds and Money market fund as seen above. But not sure having all these cash like bonds in taxable is good.

2. 401k bonds choice: I have always been using the Total US bond index funds in 401k and so thinking now if this is the right approach or should switch to any short or medium term bonds? I have access to Brokeragelink in 401k so I can get any bond etf for at least 50% of my 401k allocation, and hence this question.

3. Kids education: We have one 529 worth 60k now (not included in the above portfolio), and planning of contributing for few more years and use it for younger kid's college. For older one: planning to cashflow if we are employed. If not then we'll split the existing 529 for them and rest will be student loans. Is there any better option for this, as I think 529 contribution is too late for older one who is in high school now.

4. Any other suggestions or comments. Anything we haven't considered?
1) To be a little pedantic, "taxable bond" is a technical term that means a bond subject to tax, not "bonds in taxable." So you have no taxable bonds in taxable you say "cash like bonds" but SUB has a duration of 1.95 and VTEB of 7.32. Even factoring in your MMF, your overall duration of your bonds in taxable is 3.1 years. So you have more duration than you think.

There's nothing necessarily wrong with what you are doing here. It depends how involved you want to be in watching interest rates, and your target duration. You could always shorten up by selling VTEB (likely with little/no tax consequences) closer to retirement and going into something else.

2) This depends on what you want from your bonds specifically. However I will note that, historically, an intermediate treasury fund would have been a better choice than total bond.

3) Seems fine.

4) I don't think I'd count your single stock positions as part of your retirement assets. Consider it a buffer at best.

Also, some on this site will raise an eyebrow at the plan to retire at 52 with 25x saved. This is considered a fairly early retirement and your approach to retirement will be different than the generic advice on this site. Building up a nice buffer of muni bonds will help with this, but you will at least need a flexible spending plan and a plan to access tax-sheltered accounts if needed.

‘The Incomer’ Attracted Domhnall Gleeson and Moby and Got Its Creator on the BAFTA Breakthrough List. Now, He’s Working on a Plan B Film.

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Scottish writer-director Louis Paxton takes THR through his feature debut, how much it reflects real-life experiences and what’s next for him.

Scottish writer-director Louis Paxton’s debut feature film, the dark comedy-drama The Incomer, has star power – in the form of Domhnall Gleeson (Star Wars sequel trilogy), Gayle Rankin (House of the Dragon), John Hannah (The Mummy franchise) and others. It also has an oddball, quirky vibe. And it has the backing of Focus Features, Universal Pictures International and Moby.

What else can you ask for!? Well, on Wednesday, the up-and-coming creative was unveiled as being among the names featured on the 2026 U.K. BAFTA Breakthrough list, supported by Netflix. Related Stories Movies 'Ish' Has Resonated Widely Amid "Authoritarian Turn," Says Its BAFTA Breakthrough Writer-Director News BAFTA Breakthrough U.K.: Ella Bruccoleri, Sophie Stone, Izuka Hoyle Among 21 Rising Creatives

The Incomer is set on a remote Scottish island and follows siblings who have lived in peaceful isolation for decades, hunting seabirds, chatting to mythic creatures, and defending their isle from dreaded Incomers. Their world is upended with the arrival of Daniel (Gleeson), an awkward council worker, tasked with moving them to the mainland.

Paxton talked to THR about the star-studded offbeat movie, how Moby got involved, his hopes for the BAFTA Breakthrough experience and what else is next for him.

“I’m thrilled to be part of BAFTA Breakthrough, and I’m really excited to meet the cohort, particularly all the other creatives from all of these different mediums, including games,” the filmmaker tells THR. “It will also be great to have the chance just to sit with people who are more experienced than me and have been working for a while, just ask them about their career and how it works.”

The Incomer is infused with a feel and stories he knows. “My mother’s family is from the Northern Isles of Scotland. They’re from Orkney,” Paxton tells THR. “I grew up in Edinburgh, but my mother moved back there about 10 years ago. We grew up going to these islands, which are unique. They are a very beautiful, quite remote part of Scotland, but have a very specific identity and culture.”

For example? “I went to other islands where people genuinely survived by eating birds,” he shares. “And at some point, these people had to leave, and I was just fascinated by this. What a big decision that would be if that was where your family had lived their entire lives and your heritage was that. Even if it was going to make your life better, how hard would it be to leave that behind?”

Was his youth as surreal as some of the scenes in the film? “I would say about me and my sister that there are parts of the sibling relationship that made it into the film,” Paxton explains. “When we were kids, we really knew how to push each other’s buttons and annoy each other, and so things could escalate from zero to 100 really quickly. And we definitely had some terrible fights when we were young.”

The creative credits casting director Kharmel Cochrane with bringing together a dream ensemble. “We wanted to work with as many Scottish actors as we could, and if they weren’t Scottish, that they were very good at doing a Scottish accent,” he recalls. “And thankfully Domhnall does an exceptional Scottish accent.”

His key to attracting all this talent was the script. “I spent a long time working on the script,” Paxton highlights. “And it was really reassuring in this age of IP and big projects and really humbling that a complete nobody, who’d only made short films, could write a script and that actors would want to be part of it just on the strength of the script.”

So, how did Moby’s production company Little Walnut Productions end up backing The Incomer? “They had been funding films and making films that were largely activist films,” Paxton says. “And then they’d widened their remit to include films that embodied the values that they had as people. They read The Incomer and just really liked it.”

Did Daniel being vegan have to do with that? “It’s funny because people have asked if the veganism in the film came after. Did I add that after Moby came on board?” Paxton says with a laugh. “But it wasn’t. It was already a big part of the film, and I think maybe he appreciated that. But Moby and [creative partner] Lindsay [Hicks] liked the film generally. And they were the best partners to work with – just excellent and really supportive.”

Can fans of The Incomer expect more from Paxton ahead? “It’s all about film for me, film and TV,” he tells THR. “I’ve always wanted to be a filmmaker. I’ve signed with CAA in the U.S., and I’ve been having really exciting conversations about my next projects. I’m writing and directing two projects which are U.K.-set.”

And he can share this much: “One’s a horror comedy, and the other is more of a straight comedy. But I’m also attached to direct a film called The Waffle House Index, which is a Plan B project,” meaning from Brad Pitt’s production company, in which French company Mediawan owns a majority stake.“We’ve got Inde Navarrette as the lead, and Apple are the studio we’re making it with, and we’re really excited about that,” Paxton says. “That’ll be shooting next year. I think that’s most likely to be my next project. I didn’t write it; Andrew Nunnelly wrote the script. It was on the Black List and is a terrific script – really original, really different. It feels totally in line with the kind of projects or films that people are really responding to at the moment. I’m so excited to be a part of that.”

Novartis: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Operating margins are on track to hit 40% by 2029 as cost discipline accelerates alongside new drug launches.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Oct. 27, 2026, Novartis (NVS -1.40%) raised its full-year 2026 core operating income guidance after delivering 4% net sales growth. This performance matters for long-term investors because the pharmaceutical giant is demonstrating that expanding sales from recently launched drugs can successfully replace revenue from older medicines losing patent protection. Three key insights from management explain how the company is maintaining its growth trajectory.

Novartis raised its full-year 2026 core operating income guidance, which tracks non-GAAP (adjusted) operating profit before one-time items, to in-line to low-single-digit growth, up from a previously projected decline. This operational shift shows that rising sales of priority medicines are offsetting patent expirations for key legacy treatments.

Management upgraded peak sales projections for key pipeline drugs, including Rhapsido for skin conditions such as chronic spontaneous urticaria (CSU), where hives appear without clear triggers. Promising trial results for remibrutinib in relapsing multiple sclerosis (RMS), a central nervous system disorder, further reinforce the company's ability to replace aging therapies.

Selling, general, and administrative (SG&A) expenses, which cover marketing and operational overhead, fell 7% during the third quarter. Lower third-party spending and efficiency gains from technology are driving progress toward a target of 40% core operating margin by 2029.

Sales momentum across newly launched drugs is outpacing legacy revenue losses, and management is openly highlighting this replacement capability. The company benefits from commercial scale across international markets and a deep pipeline, while management tracks patent litigation outcomes and pricing discipline.

Capital allocation remains focused on steady dividend growth and targeted share repurchases, supported by free cash flow generation. The long-term thesis hinges on whether late-stage clinical readouts maintain this momentum over the coming years.

US midterm election results: How Americans voted and what the data tells us

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Results are starting to come in from across the US, where people voted on their next Congress. DW takes a look at who voted Republican, who voted Democrat and what it all means.

We will update this article throughout the night as more data becomes available, so keep checking back.

In the 2026 midterms, Americans voted on all 435 members of the House of Representatives and 35 members of the Senate. Before election night, Republicans held the majority in both Congress chambers, if only by a hair. In 36 US states, voters also decided on who will become the next governor.

Midterm elections are held every four years, at the exact halfway point between presidential elections. The results will influence US politics for the next few years. Despite the significant executive power delegated to the presidency according to the US Constitution, the majority of federal political decisions have to be approved by Congress before they can be implemented. If the Democrats win the majority in one or both chambers of Congress, it will be harder for President Donald Trump to govern as he chooses.

Because the 50 US states lie across six time zones in total, results will trickle in over time, starting with the US East Coast and going west from there. DW follows the Associated Press (AP) for when a seat is called for Republicans or Democrats.

Polling stations in several eastern states closed at 1 a.m. local time. For more updates, go to our US midterms live blog.

In the US Senate, Democrats retained several of their seats on the East Coast, with Virginia being the first to be called. North Carolina was the first Republican Senate seat, which the Democrats were able to flip.

In the House of Representatives, all 435 seats are up for election.

The data on voter demographics and attitudes we use is based on a representative survey of more than 75,000 verified voters conducted by survey and market research firm SSRS on behalf of AP and other news organizations. It builds on interviews of both early and mail-in voters as well as exit polls of in-person voters on Election Day, November 3. Where percentages of Republican and Democratic voters don't add up to 100%, the remaining percent were voters who voted for third-party candidates.

One of the most important factors in this year's midterms is the economy. Prices for gas have increased significantly since the start of the Iran war, and everyday goods have become more and more expensive across the country as well. Almost half of all Americans say their family's financial situation is worse than two years ago.

That sentiment is not the same across the board though. Among those saying their family is better off than two years ago, 75% voted Republican and 23% Democrat. Among those saying they are worse off, only 24% voted Republican and 72% Democrat. President Trump has been saying that the country is on the upswing.

While not on the ballot, Trump is still a strong presence in the midterms: The midterms are considered a referendum on how he and his Republicans governed the country for the past two years. Most recently, Trump's approval ratings have fallen to the lowest he has ever had, with just under 30% of Americans saying they think he is doing a good job.

In recent history, only George W. Bush has fared worse, with 25% at the end of his second term. During the 2006 midterm election, Bush's Republican Party lost control of both chambers of Congress.

Exit polls showed that half of voters saw the 2026 midterm elections as an opportunity to rebuke the president. Three-quarters of US voters said they are "angry" or "dissatisfied" with the way the country is headed.

According to the AP Voter Poll, dissatisfaction was very high in Democratic-leaning states such as California and Massachusetts. However, even in Republican-leaning states, a majority of voters were at least "dissatisfied."

Before Tuesday's election, Republicans (and independents who caucus with them) held 53 of 100 seats in the Senate and 220 of 435 seats in the House. That means they only held two more seats than the 218 required for a majority. In recent years, such razor-thin majorities have been the lay of the land, especially in the House of Representatives.

Dragon Ball's New Anime Proves That Toei Is Holding The Franchise Back

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

People who grew up loving Dragon Ball are currently able to enjoy the weekly releases of the Japanese version of Dragon Ball Super: Beerus series, but the hype for the improved anime could be higher. Revised animations, pacing, and entirely new sequences have made the newest release more than a standard remaster. But that hasn't stopped Toei, the anime's producers, from making the same mistakes it's been repeating for more than 13 years.

The Battle of Gods movie and Dragon Ball Super anime series helped make Akira Toriyama's franchise more popular than ever. Their dramatic success proved the world still had a big appetite for more adventures with Goku and his friends. And while the Dragon Ball Super: Broly and Super Hero movies delivered in many ways, those who control the rights and production schedules of the beloved franchise have failed to capitalize on that success, holding the franchise back from reaching its full potential.

During the premiere of Dragon Ball Super's final episodes, which featured the incredibly epic conclusion of the universal Tournament of Power, fans flooded the streets of major cities around the world to cheer and enjoy the series together. The anime garnered that sort of reception with the release of the Japanese version of the series, but one can't help but wonder if the hype would have been even greater if the series' largest markets released their dubs at the same time in movie theaters.

Judging by how the world's most popular anime streaming platform, Crunchyroll, has experienced crashes during several anticipated Dragon Ball Super moments, it's clear that a lot of fans around the world are more than willing to watch the series with subtitles. However, there is still a large percentage of hardcore Dragon Ball fans who prefer to wait and watch the franchise's newest releases with the iconic voice actors they grew up with. Unfortunately for them, some of the wait times for those dubs have been much longer than others.

The Japanese language version of the Dragon Ball Super anime premiered in July 2015, with the English version not releasing until January 2017. That was a considerable amount of time for arguably the world's most popular anime series to release its English dub, one of the franchise's largest markets/demographics. Because of the separate release schedules, hype for new releases can be hindered, compared to if Toei made sure the largest markets around the world had their dubs ready so that new Dragon Ball content could be released at the same time in multiple languages.

It's taken little time for the newest Dragon Ball anime to prove that it's more than a remaster, it's a drastic improvement in all the best ways.

Since the Dragon Ball Super anime ended, there have been two movies and one completely separate anime series, Dragon Ball: Daima, released. While the movies made hundreds of millions of dollars at the worldwide box office, Daima received a much more lukewarm reception. Unsurprisingly, all of those releases saw the Japanese version released first, with the English versions of the movies releasing only around a month later. And while English fans did not need to wait as long as they did for the Dragon Ball Super dub, Daima's dub took an extra three months to begin streaming.

Dragon Ball’s current fragmented release model holds it back from even greater heights. Toei is repeating that mistake with Dragon Ball Super: Beerus, which began releasing on Crunchyroll on October 11, 2026. The English dub is likely being worked on as I type this article, but its release date has yet to be announced. And unless Toei changes its current production processes, the highly anticipated Dragon Ball Super: Galactic Patrol arc will also suffer from an uncoordinated launch. And that isn't the only way that Toei is hurting the franchise's potential.

Newer anime series like Jujutsu Kaisen and Demon Slayer have dramatically altered the increasingly popular anime medium in several beneficial and lucrative ways. While their incredible quality and pacing have helped them stand out from many other anime productions, their theatrical releases have both earned their distributors millions of dollars in profit and been used to greatly increase the world's excitement for their anime series' returns. Both qualities that Toei is not currently utilizing with Dragon Ball.

The producers of Demon Slayer's anime have created the perfect format for milking a franchise for all it's worth. Before the decision to make the series' final arc into three separate movies, they helped open Pandora's box with the release of the 2020 film Demon Slayer The Movie: Mugen Train. After the first season’s huge success, millions of fans worldwide hotly anticipated its continuation. When the next section of the story was released as a two-hour movie, that large fanbase was more than anxious to pay for a movie ticket to see the next part on the big screen.

Almost six months later, Demon Slayer's second season finally began releasing on Crunchyroll. It also covered the Mugen Train arc but with additional content and scenes that could not fit in the movie. Fans who were able to see that part of the story in the theaters were more than anxious to share their experience online, which generated even more hype for the anime's return for those who were not able to see the movie in theaters. It was a brilliant business tactic, but future theatrical anime releases would not execute this strategy nearly as effectively.

One of my biggest movie theater disappointments in 2025 was Jujutsu Kaisen: Execution. The series' Shibuya arc remains one of the best seasons of anime to ever release, but some of its greatest battles were unfinished. Its physical release was able to add those missing sections, and Jujutsu Kaisen: Execution could have used those as a way to entice fans to the theater to see the fights as they were intended. Instead, it was an underwhelming summary of the Shibuya arc with a few minutes of the anticipated continuation tagged on at the end. Still, that release made over $40 million.

If Toei wanted to, they could use these lessons to make every Dragon Ball anime release a worldwide event that would take box offices by storm and further generate excitement for its new series. While Dragon Ball: Daima ended up being a disappointment for some, I'm sure others would have joined me at the movie theater if Toei had released the first 4–5 episodes of that series or Dragon Ball Super: Beerus in theaters a few months before they started streaming.

Releases like Demon Slayer: Mugen Train both respected audiences and helped build excitement for the anime series' return, and Toei could do the same with Dragon Ball. On the other hand, regurgitating already released content for an easy cash-in, as Jujutsu Kaisen has, would likely disappoint certain fans like myself. If Toei is willing to change their current Dragon Ball production processes, hopefully they can find a happy medium that will allow even more fans to get excited about each new release while also making the already profitable franchise even more so.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Park Hoon-jung’s Assassin Noir ‘Tristes Tropiques’ Sells to North America, France, Spain

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

“Tristes Tropiques,” the latest genre outing from “New World” and “The Witch” director Park Hoon-jung, has been picked up for North America by Well Go USA as Finecut, which is carrying the film in its AFM line-up, closes a fresh round of deals ahead of the film’s Nov. 25 theatrical release in Korea.

Swift Distribution, whose president and CEO Didier Costet called the film “an exciting new cinematic journey,” has acquired French-speaking territories. Alfa Pictures will handle Spain and Andorra, Blue Swan Entertainment has Italy and CaiChang International, Inc. has taken Taiwan. Related Stories Sega's 'Sonic the Hedgehog' to Launch Lingokids Games (EXCLUSIVE)

The new pacts build on sales unveiled earlier to Splendid Film for Germany, Austria and Switzerland; Media4Fun for Poland; Multivision India for the Indian subcontinent; PT Prima Cinema Multimedia for Indonesia; and Izagur Media for Mongolia.

The story centers on a band of young killers trained by Master, who holds absolute sway over a tropical rainforest. Then Master turns up dead. Nobody knows who killed him, and the assassins begin to distrust one another.

The cast features Lee Sin-young (“Rebound”), Park Yu-rim (“Drive My Car”), Kim Myung-min (“Your Honor”) and Park Hae-soo (“Squid Game”). Taiwanese actors Austin Lin (“96 Minutes”), Chen Yi-Wen (“The Pig, the Snake and the Pigeon”) and Wang Po-Chieh (“Eye of the Storm”) are also part of the ensemble.

Well Go USA has a track record with the filmmaker, having released “The Witch” films and “The Childe” in North America. Doris Pfardrescher, the company’s president and CEO, said: “We’re thrilled to continue our relationship with Park Hoon-jung. ‘Tristes Tropiques’ takes his signature blend of style, emotion, and hard-hitting action to a whole new level.”

Park’s other credits include “Night in Paradise” for Netflix and “The Tyrant” for Disney+. Gold Moon Pictures, Park’s own company, produced the film, with Studio&New co-producing. Mindmark Inc., the Shinsegae Group’s creative content studio, presents.

The film has already won over genre festivals abroad. The Brussels International Fantastic Film Festival gave it the Silver Raven Award from its International Competition, and in September Paris’ L’Étrange Festival named it winner of its Grand Prix Nouveau Genre Canal+. It has also been chosen to close the London Korean Film Festival.

Finecut is also bringing animated feature “Long Long Night” to AFM, where an English-dubbed version will get its first market screening. The film’s festival run includes a Toronto Film Festival premiere and a Busan International Film Festival screening.

Both cards are useful for American Airlines flyers, but one offers more flexibility.

Airline miles, hotel points, travel insurance

This page includes information about these cards, currently unavailable on NerdWallet. The information has been collected by NerdWallet and has not been provided or reviewed by the card issuer.

Both the Citi® / AAdvantage® Platinum Select® World Elite Mastercard®  and Citi Strata Premier® Card can help you rack up AAdvantage miles. The former earns them directly; the latter transfers points to American Airlines at a 1:1 ratio. So which one is better for American Airlines flyers?

For earning rewards, the Citi Strata Premier® Card is the winner. Although it's not an American Airlines credit card, it can make it easier to accumulate AAdvantage miles on many purchases with its higher earning rates and the option to transfer ThankYou points to American.

Still, the Citi® / AAdvantage® Platinum Select® World Elite Mastercard® can help you achieve AAdvantage elite status and offers perks when flying American Airlines, including a free checked bag and preferred boarding. In 2027, the annual fee will increase, but the card will add new bonus categories and other benefits.

The Citi Strata Premier® Card is a better fit for travelers who want to earn American Airlines miles without committing all their rewards to a single airline program.

Although the Citi® / AAdvantage® Platinum Select® World Elite Mastercard® is an American Airlines credit card, the Citi Strata Premier® Card can be a faster way to accumulate AAdvantage miles on many purchases.

That's because Citi Strata Premier® Card holders can transfer ThankYou points to American Airlines AAdvantage — and several other loyalty programs — at a 1:1 ratio.

Currently, the Citi Strata Premier® Card offers more rewards than the Citi® / AAdvantage® Platinum Select® World Elite Mastercard® in every bonus category. On Jan. 24, 2027, when the benefits on the Citi® / AAdvantage® Platinum Select® World Elite Mastercard® change, that will change — but only in a couple of narrowly defined categories: select streaming services and eligible Rides and Rails purchases, which include taxis, rideshares, subways and trains. Unless those categories make up a significant portion of your spending, the Citi Strata Premier® Card will likely yield more points overall, even if your ultimate goal is American Airlines miles.

» Learn more: 11 Benefits of the Citi Strata Premier® Card

The Citi Strata Premier® Card also offers a $100 annual hotel benefit when you book a single hotel stay of $500 or more, excluding taxes and fees, through Citi Travel. Using the full benefit can more than offset the card’s annual fee, and hotel bookings through Citi Travel earn 10 ThankYou points per dollar.

Just keep in mind that booking through Citi Travel may mean giving up hotel loyalty program benefits you would receive by booking directly. Whether the $100 hotel perk offers enough value to justify booking through Citi Travel will depend on the property, the rates available and how much you value those hotel loyalty benefits.

» Learn more: Is the Citi Strata Premier® Card worth its annual fee?

One of the biggest advantages of the Citi Strata Premier® Card is the ability to earn ThankYou points, which can be transferred to several airline and hotel loyalty programs. If you want the option to transfer points to American Airlines but also see value in the card’s other airline and hotel partners, the Citi Strata Premier® Card is a better choice.

Transfer ratios will vary depending on which card you have.

Accor Live Limitless (2:1 or 2:0.7 ratio).

The Leading Hotels of the World (5:1 or 5:0.7 ratio).

Preferred Hotels & Resorts (1:2 or 1:1.4 ratio).

» Learn more: Citi ThankYou transfer partners: A complete guide

Although you can transfer points from the Citi Strata Premier® Card to American Airlines, you won’t get any Loyalty Points.

» Learn more: Guide to American Airlines elite status

The Citi® / AAdvantage® Platinum Select® World Elite Mastercard® is a strong choice if you want American Airlines benefits. Keep in mind that the card is getting revamped in 2027 with a higher annual fee and new benefits. See NerdWallet's coverage for more details.

The Citi® / AAdvantage® Platinum Select® World Elite Mastercard® comes with several benefits that can significantly offset the annual fee:

Free checked bag: Get a free first checked bag on domestic American Airlines itineraries for the primary cardholder and up to four companions traveling on the same reservation. If you check bags with American, this benefit alone could make the card worth getting.

Preferred boarding: The primary cardholder and up to four companions on the same reservation receive preferred boarding.

American Airlines Vacations discount: Save 10% on eligible American Airlines Vacations packages.

Inflight discount: 25% off inflight food and beverage purchases. This perk is being replaced on Jan. 24, 2027, with an inflight statement credit.

$125 flight discount: Spend at least $20,000 during your card membership year and renew the card to receive a $125 American Airlines flight discount. This perk is also changing in 2027; see below.

Starting Jan. 24, 2027, the card will offer:

Inflight statement credits: Receive up to $100 back in statement credits annually on inflight purchases. This replaces the old 25% off inflight food and drink purchases.

$150 flight discount: Spend at least $20,000 during your card membership year and renew the card to receive a $150 American Airlines flight discount. This replaces the $125 flight discount; the spending requirement is the same.

The inflight statement credits, in particular, will be easy to maximize and could potentially offset a meaningful portion of the card’s annual fee. Combined with the ability to earn Loyalty Points toward elite status and the card’s other perks, that can make the card more valuable for American’s flyers.

» Learn more: Is an American Airlines credit card worth it?

The Citi® / AAdvantage® Platinum Select® World Elite Mastercard® offers Turo credits. You receive up to $180 annually in statement credits on eligible Turo rentals, with up to $30 back per eligible rental.

On Jan. 24, 2027, the card will add several more perks:

Walmart+: Primary cardholders receive a complimentary annual Walmart+ membership, valued at $98.

Global Entry or TSA PreCheck: Receive up to a $120 application fee credit every four years.

FOX One: Get 50% off an annual FOX One subscription.

Travel protections: Get trip delay protection, trip cancellation and interruption protection, worldwide car rental insurance and lost baggage protection.

It’s unusual to see a Global Entry or TSA PreCheck credit on a card with an annual fee under $200. When that perk kicks in, the card could become a good option for travelers who prioritize AAdvantage miles but also want a credit card that fully reimburses the application fee for Global Entry or TSA PreCheck.

» Learn more: American Airlines credit card benefits guide

Cardholders receive 1 Loyalty Point for every eligible AAdvantage mile earned from purchases. That means everyday spending can move you closer to AAdvantage elite status, in addition to earning miles you can redeem for flights. American’s entry-level elite status requires 40,000 Loyalty Points, so putting significant spending on the card can help you reach that threshold.

If you want to earn rewards on everyday spending and have the flexibility to move those points to American Airlines — and you don’t need the airline-specific benefits — the Citi Strata Premier® Card is the best choice.

That said, the Citi® / AAdvantage® Platinum Select® World Elite Mastercard® makes more sense for frequent American Airlines flyers who can take advantage of benefits such as a free checked bag and preferred boarding, or for travelers who want to earn Loyalty Points toward American Airlines elite status through credit card spending.

Why Fiserv Stock Triumphed on Tuesday

📅 Nov 04, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

The company showed signs of a potential turnaround in its third-quarter earnings.

Fiserv (FISV +12.46%), one of the sturdier and more experienced fintechs on the market, had a Tuesday to remember as far as its equity was concerned. After it served up an encouraging quarterly earnings report in the morning, investors eagerly bought the company's shares to send them almost 13% higher across that trading session.

Fiserv's third-quarter revenue not under generally accepted accounting principles (non-GAAP, or adjusted) slid by 1% year over year to $4.85 billion. Despite the slide, that figure was essentially in line with the consensus analyst estimate.

As for profitability, adjusted net income fell more steeply, decreasing by 15% to $936 million, or $1.75 per share. Those Fiserv-tracking pundits were expecting a slightly sharper fall, since they were collectively estimating $1.72.

Fiserv said in its earnings release that the erosion in revenue it suffered in its key banking customer segment was improving notably. Clients didn't migrate away from Fiserv back-end solutions as much as they had in other recent quarters, while the company reported "major client wins" in that business.

Investors were also heartened by management's doubling of its Project Elevate cost-saving and automation target to $1 billion from $500 million. Another confidence booster was the company's news that it would resume its share repurchase program, with a goal of buying back $500 million worth of stock in the current (fourth) quarter alone.

Fiserv maintained its full-year 2026 organic revenue guidance of a slump of 1% to break-even when matched against the previous year's figure. It's also sticking to its adjusted EPS forecast of $7.20 to $7.40, which is well down from the $8.64 it posted in 2025.

While no investor likes to see declines in either trailing results or guidance, Fiserv's moves position it for renewed growth in the near future. So now feels like it might be an opportune time to buy into the company's (hopefully continuing) recovery story.
